\begin{abstract}
Relative to the entire qualified geometric cone grant G331, we reconstruct
the current normal-surface theorem and prove envelope equivalence under
bijective normalization boundary restriction, including explicit radicial
conductor quotient criteria. Numerical-semigroup cusp germs have all
continuous envelopes in every dimension over an algebraically closed
characteristic-zero field; the surface scope is characteristic free.
An explicit cancellation-sensitive cusp obstacle has every-degree uniform
canonical error at most $2/m$ in every characteristic and, in characteristic
zero, a non-finitely-generated canonical algebra. A regular threefold actual
primary model supplies a curve of degree $-1$ for an ideal's divisorial part,
pinpointing the failure of the surface competitor method. The general
excellent higher-dimensional normal and conductor problem remains open.
Compilation, publication and acceptance are reserved to ROOT.
\end{abstract}

\[
A_m(f)=\{g:v(g)+mf(v)\geq0\ \forall v\},\quad

H_N-\frac{NM_1}{m}\leq h_m\leq p(f),\qquad

z^*=\max\{z:z\leq\min(d,0),\ Qz\geq0\},\qquad

\section{n2. Full conductor-envelope equivalence when the entire normalized boundary is bijective; explicit radicial and primary-conductor criteria.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1.}

New conductor theorem, relative to ENTIRE qualified G331 and own n1. Let R be excellent reduced local with finite normalization S and conductor c. Set X=V\_a(R) and Xtilde=V\_(aS)(S), using every closed normalization centre and EVERY semivaluation support. Suppose restriction on the conductor quotient boundaries is injective. More precisely, the boundary of (S/c)\_red maps injectively to that of (R/c)\_red, with the quotient of the extended gauge, omitting empty zero-dimensional sections. Then rho:Xtilde-\textgreater{}X is a homeomorphism. Indeed G331 grants surjectivity and compact Hausdorff quotient topology. At finite conductor value every fibre is singleton. At infinite value its ENTIRE fibre is precisely the conductor-quotient restriction fibre. The assumed injectivity handles all those fibres. Compact bijectivity gives the homeomorphism. This is a sufficient condition, not an asserted necessity for radiciality of an algebraic conductor map.

Here are two algebraically checkable sufficient cases. First, if R/c has dimension zero, c contains a power of m, so every centred semivaluation has finite conductor value. No quotient boundary remains; multiple normalization centres are allowed. Second, let B=(R/c)\_red and T=(S/c)\_red. Assume each prime p of B has exactly one prime P of T above it, and the finite field extension Frac(T/P)/Frac(B/p) is purely inseparable (in characteristic zero this means equal). These are the usual finite radicial conditions, stated explicitly. The reduction map B-\textgreater{}T is injective: nilpotence upstairs of an element of R/c is already nilpotence in its injected subring. For a quotient semivaluation with support p, any lift has support P, uniquely. After quotienting the supports, its value on any nonzero field element x is forced by w(x)=v(x\textasciicircum{}(p\textasciicircum{}e))/p\textasciicircum{}e when an appropriate power lies in the old field; in characteristic zero it is already the same field. This proves uniqueness on every support, not just generic valuations. Existence is G331 finite lifting. Thus the quotient boundary is injective. No global common Frobenius exponent, perfection, F-finiteness or characteristic-zero restriction is required for this uniqueness proof. We do not invent a real field valuation through a nonzero support.

Because EVERY rho-fibre is singleton, G331's FULL positive C and D descent implies order bijections rho*:C\_R-\textgreater{}C\_S and rho*:D\_R-\textgreater{}D\_S. On C this is an isometry, since rho is surjective; continuity in either direction follows from the homeomorphism. Thus, for ANY obstacle for which the raw supremum is interpreted pointwise, the completed order bijection gives p\_S(rho*f)=rho*p\_R(f). For finite continuous signed obstacles the raw suprema are finite by n1. The continuous-envelope property is EQUIVALENT on R and S: every continuous source obstacle is the pullback of a continuous target obstacle, and an envelope belongs to C on one side iff it does on the other. The descended continuous envelope is independently geometric by positive descent, not by an unsupported assertion that a pointwise supremum is positive.

The canonical degree-m ideals satisfy A\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f)=A\_m\textasciicircum{}S(rho*f) intersect R, directly from surjective restriction and ALL defining inequalities. Equality after extension to S is NOT asserted. When CE holds, n1 gives uniform convergence in EVERY downstairs canonical degree. Each such profile has an ACTUAL negative effective Cartier presentation -E\_(A\_m)/m on its ACTUAL primary blowup; its degree on EVERY contracted integral curve is positive by ampleness. Consequently the uniform geometric envelope has actual positive approximants on the required model system. A continuous envelope need not itself have a single-model determination. Neither a source O(1/m) bound nor source finite generation is automatically inherited through contraction; n3 establishes an explicit rate and failure of finite generation for a particular family by a different argument.

The exact primary-gauge cocycle commutes with rho since w(aS)=v(a). For a new gauge b, d(v)=v(b), source and target divide by the same bounded positive denominator. Canonical ideals remain unchanged when the obstacle is transported homogeneously. Completion follows G331 and n1. For decreasing continuous obstacles with continuous envelopes, the actual D rule of n1 transports as well, with partial bottom and bottom retained. This theorem supplies envelope descent only under the stated singleton-fibre criterion; it does not settle arbitrary nonnormal gluing along a positive-dimensional conductor quotient.

Consequences: any excellent reduced local germ whose conductor is primary has CE exactly when all of its normal local centres have CE. In particular, the dimension-two normal theorem of n1 gives CE for every such germ whose normal centres all have dimension at most two. Under the finite radicial conductor-quotient condition the same equivalence holds even when the infinite-conductor boundary is NONEMPTY. If S is a normal local surface it has CE in any equal/mixed characteristic by n1; if its centres are smooth affine closed germs over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero field it has CE in every dimension by n1. These are narrowed conclusions with explicit source hypotheses, not added premises for unrestricted ORIGINAL P12.3.

The point where the unrestricted descent problem remains is now precise: for a quotient boundary with nonsingleton fibres there is no automatic constancy of a source greatest minorant. This note neither takes a fibrewise minimum as psh nor asserts arbitrary finite-map envelope compatibility. Products, energy and full finite operators remain separate.

This is a mathematical proof using G331 only for its qualified boundary/cone/descent results and n1 for envelopes. No new published premise is needed for the radicial uniqueness argument. \subsection*{Expanded conductor theorem}
With $\mathfrak c=(R:S)$, assume the reduced conductor quotient boundary
restriction is injective. Then
\[
\rho:V_{\mathfrak aS}(S)\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\mathfrak a}(R),\quad
\rho^*C_R=C_S,\quad\rho^*D_R=D_S,
\]
\[
\rho^*p_R(f)=p_S(\rho^*f),\qquad
\operatorname{CE}(R)\Longleftrightarrow\operatorname{CE}(S),\qquad
A_m^R=A_m^S\cap R.
\]
This condition includes the entire infinite-conductor support stratum.
The last identity is contraction only, not an extension identity.


\section{n3. Nonisolated nonnormal CE in every dimension over algebraically closed characteristic zero; explicit all-characteristic cusp envelope error \textless{}=2/m and char-zero non-FG.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1, n2.}

New higher-dimensional nonnormal continuous-envelope sector and explicit quantitative example, using ENTIRE qualified G331 and own n1--n2. Let H be a proper numerical semigroup (a finitely generated additive subsemigroup of the nonnegative integers containing zero with finite complement) and let c be its conductor integer: every integer \textgreater{}=c belongs to H and c-1 does not. For any field k and d\textgreater{}=2, set R\_H=k[t\textasciicircum{}h (h in H),z\_1,...,z\_(d-1)] localized at the origin, and S=k[t,z\_1,...,z\_(d-1)] localized there. Both rings are excellent finite-type domains of dimension d. S is finite over R\_H since t is integral (some positive t\textasciicircum{}h lies downstairs), and their fraction fields coincide because gcd(H)=1. S is regular and integrally closed, hence is the normalization. There is just one centre over the origin. Its conductor is exactly t\textasciicircum{}c S. To verify this on polynomials, all terms of t-degree \textgreater{}=c multiply into the semigroup ring. If a polynomial has a term of degree h\textless{}c, multiply by t\textasciicircum{}(c-1-h); its coefficient of the forbidden degree c-1 remains nonzero. Distinct t-degrees cannot cancel it. Conductor commutes with localization because S/R\_H is finite. The reduced conductor quotients are BOTH k[z\_1,...,z\_(d-1)] at the origin, and their map is the identity.

The infinite-conductor boundary is nonempty: a valuation killing t and centered on the z-ring belongs to it. Thus n2 proves a full boundary homeomorphism including an actual positive-dimensional-support conductor stratum. In characteristic zero over algebraically closed k, the exact smooth source theorem of n1 gives a greatest continuous independently geometric C/D envelope for EVERY finite continuous signed obstacle on EVERY R\_H, in EVERY dimension d. Uniform convergence holds in every canonical degree and for every primary gauge. These are nonnormal nonisolated germs: the normalization fails along t=0, a locus of dimension d-1, because t remains integral but missing at its generic point. The result goes beyond normal smooth finite quotients; it is not inferred from characteristic-zero resolution of these singular germs.

For d=2 the source is a normal surface, so the same all-continuous-obstacle theorem holds over EVERY field, including imperfect fields and positive characteristic. There is also a mixed-characteristic example R=Z\_p[[t\textasciicircum{}2,t\textasciicircum{}3]]=Z\_p[[a,b]]/(b\textasciicircum{}2-a\textasciicircum{}3), S=Z\_p[[t]]. Grouping even/odd powers proves the indicated embedding and finiteness; S is complete regular of dimension two, its fraction field agrees, and t\textasciicircum{}2 S is the conductor. The reduced conductor quotients are both Z\_p. They have a nonempty centred boundary (the p-order ray), so infinite-conductor supports have not been discarded. N2 plus the normal-surface theorem proves CE for EVERY continuous signed obstacle here. Higher-dimensional positive/mixed-characteristic smooth source CE remains unproved by this argument.

For H=\textless{}2,3\textgreater{} there is a new explicit all-support example in EVERY dimension d\textgreater{}=2 and EVERY field, independent of any general source CE theorem. Use gauge a=(t\textasciicircum{}2,t\textasciicircum{}3,z\_1,...,z\_(d-1)), b=z\_1+t and J=(b,t\textasciicircum{}2,z\_2,...,z\_(d-1)) in S. J is primary. By the homeomorphism define the continuous downstairs A(v)=w(J), where rho(w)=v, and obstacle f=-A. For integers m\textgreater{}=1 form the ACTUAL downstairs primary ideal
K\_m=(b\textasciicircum{}m(z\_1-mt), t\textasciicircum{}2 b\textasciicircum{}m, t\textasciicircum{}(2m+2))+(z\_2,...,z\_(d-1))\textasciicircum{}m.
When d=2 omit the last summand and put U=+infinity below. The first generator has no t-linear coefficient: m z\_1\textasciicircum{}m-m z\_1\textasciicircum{}m=0, including in characteristic dividing m. All its other t-degrees are zero or \textgreater{}=2, so it lies in R. The remaining generators lie there too. The radical contains t\textasciicircum{}2,t\textasciicircum{}3,z\_1 and all the other z\_j, hence K\_m is proper primary. No generator is treated as a supplied executable expression.

Here is the universal bound, including EVERY support and infinite coordinate. Write s=w(t), u=w(z\_1), B=w(b), U=min\_(j\textgreater{}=2)w(z\_j), so min(2s,u,U)=1 and A=min(B,2s,U). A is finite. Every displayed generator has value \textgreater{}=mA, hence w(K\_m)\textgreater{}=mA. If U=A, the added ideal has value mU=mA, giving equality. Otherwise A\textless{}U. If 2s\textless{}=B, then s is finite and u=s: unequal values would force B=min(s,u)\textless{}2s. Since U\textgreater{}2s and min(2s,s,U)=1, s=u=1. The last original generator then has value mA+2. In the remaining case B\textless{}2s, A=B\textless{}U. If s\textless{}=u, then s is finite; B\textgreater{}=s. If s\textgreater{}1 all of 2s,u,U would exceed one, a contradiction. Thus s\textless{}=1, and t\textasciicircum{}2 b\textasciicircum{}m has value mA+2s\textless{}=mA+2. If s\textgreater{}u (including s=infinity), B=w(z\_1-mt)=u: for m nonzero in k, m is a unit and the values are unequal, and if m=0 in k the second expression is simply z\_1. The normalization and U\textgreater{}u give u=1, so the first generator has value mA+1. These cases exhaust all possibilities, including B=infinity, U=infinity, killed coordinates and cancellation. Consequently
0\textless{}=v(K\_m)/m-A(v)\textless{}=2/m on the ENTIRE normalized boundary.

Therefore f is a uniform limit from below of -v(K\_m)/m. Each profile is the evaluation of ACTUAL -E\_(K\_m)/m on ACTUAL Bl\_(K\_m)(R), negative effective and relatively ample, with positive degree on EVERY contracted integral curve. Thus f belongs to independently geometric C even in higher positive characteristic where general CE is unknown. It is its own greatest C/D minorant. Since K\_m subset A\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f), the canonical profiles themselves obey the EVERY-degree uniform estimate 0\textless{}=p(f)-h\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f)\textless{}=2/m. This establishes a quantitative geometric envelope across the infinite-conductor stratum without a conductor-value/N error or a root-map assumption.

In characteristic ZERO f is outside the ENTIRE finite REAL raw negative ideal cone Q\_R, and its canonical algebra is NOT finitely generated. Define w\_infinity by killing t,z\_2,... and giving z\_1 value one; define w\_star by killing z\_2,... and using monomial weights (t,b)=(1,2), with z\_1=b-t. Both satisfy the chosen gauge, and A(w\_infinity)=1, A(w\_star)=2. For ANY proper primary downstairs ideal I, its image after killing the other z\_j is primary in k[t\textasciicircum{}2,t\textasciicircum{}3,z\_1]. Let e=w\_infinity(I)\textgreater{}0. A generator attaining e has a unit denominator at both valuations. Its formal expansion has no t-degree-one terms, has nonzero z\_1\textasciicircum{}e coefficient, and has zero t z\_1\textasciicircum{}(e-1) coefficient. Its total-degree-e homogeneous part therefore cannot be a scalar multiple of b\textasciicircum{}e in characteristic zero. After z\_1=b-t it contains some t\textasciicircum{}(e-j)b\textasciicircum{}j with j\textless{}e, of w\_star-value e+j\textless{}=2e-1. Other total degrees cannot cancel this monomial. Thus w\_star(I)\textless{}2w\_infinity(I). Every nonzero finite nonnegative REAL combination of proper primary gauges preserves this strict inequality; unit terms vanish. A has equality, so A is outside the raw positive cone and f outside Q\_R. Finite homogeneous generation of the canonical algebra would force a rational primary envelope by the elementary argument in n1, contradicting this separation. No claim of non-finite-generation in positive characteristic follows from the characteristic-zero argument.

This is continuous-envelope regularity WITH a canonical O(1/m) bound and WITHOUT canonical finite generation for an explicit cancellation-sensitive nonnormal higher-dimensional example. It is not a counterexample to CE, nor a raw geometric-core attainment claim. Under another gauge the same homogeneously transported obstacle keeps all canonical ideals, and the bound is divided by min v(new gauge)\textgreater{}0. G331's literal radial scope distinction, curve min(f,0), nilpotent reduction and empty dimension-zero case remain unchanged.

Own finite\_diagnostics.py samples only its explicitly recorded rational weights, coherent cancellation cases and infinite-coordinate endpoints, and checks the t-linear cancellation exactly. Its output is finite diagnostic evidence, not the universal proof above. Artifacts n3.md, n3.tex, diagnostics-input.json and reports/finite-diagnostics.json; rerun python finite\_diagnostics.py; python assemble.py; python verify.py.

\subsection*{Expanded higher-dimensional cusp construction}
For a numerical semigroup $H$ of conductor $c$,
\[
R_H=k[t^H,z_1,\ldots,z_{d-1}]_0,\quad
S=k[t,z_1,\ldots,z_{d-1}]_0,\quad\mathfrak c=t^cS,
\]
\[
(R_H/\mathfrak c)_{\rm red}=(S/\mathfrak c)_{\rm red}=k[z_1,\ldots,z_{d-1}]_0.
\]
For $H=\langle2,3\rangle$, $b=z_1+t$ and
$A=w((b,t^2,z_2,\ldots,z_{d-1}))$, put
\[
K_m=(b^m(z_1-mt),\ t^2b^m,\ t^{2m+2})+(z_2,\ldots,z_{d-1})^m.
\]
The proof covers all supports and establishes
\[
0\leq \frac{v(K_m)}m-A\leq\frac2m,\qquad
0\leq p(-A)-h_m^R(-A)\leq\frac2m.
\]
In characteristic zero the two test points give $A(w_\infty)=1$,
$A(w_*)=2$, whereas every nonzero finite real positive primary combination
satisfies $u(w_*)<2u(w_\infty)$. Hence $-A\notin Q_R$ and the canonical
algebra is not finitely generated. Neither conclusion is a CE failure.


\section{n4. Explicit actual-primary threefold obstruction: generic sections on exceptional surfaces do not imply all-curve-nef divisorial parts.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1.}

Precise THREE-dimensional obstruction to the surface competitor method; this is NOT a continuous-envelope counterexample. Support: ENTIRE qualified G331 and own n1 for the finite section/ACTUAL primary presentation argument. Let R=k[x,y,z]\_(x,y,z) for ANY field k, m=(x,y,z), and I=(z)+m\textasciicircum{}2, a proper primary ideal. Blow up the origin, with exceptional E\_0=P\textasciicircum{}2\_k. Blow up the two distinct k-rational points P=[1:0:0], Q=[0:1:0] of E\_0. Their disjoint point ideals can be blown up at once; equivalently do the two point blowups successively. All charts are regular (ordinary point blowups in regular coordinates). Let Y be the result, E\_1 the strict transform of E\_0, and E\_2,E\_3 the new exceptional planes. Then E\_1=Bl\_(P,Q)(P\textasciicircum{}2), while E\_2,E\_3 are disjoint copies of P\textasciicircum{}2.

This explicit Y is an ACTUAL primary model. Its second-stage negative tautological bundle O(-E\_2-E\_3) is relatively ample for Y-\textgreater{}Bl\_m(R); this is the blowup of the disjoint union of the two point ideals, invertible/unit on the complement. The first negative tautological bundle O(-E\_0) is ample. The COMPLETE immediately read Stacks29.38.7--8 gives, for an integer a sufficiently large, O(-E\_2-E\_3-a pullback(E\_0)) ample over R. Here pullback(E\_0)=E\_1+E\_2+E\_3. Thus E=aE\_1+(a+1)E\_2+(a+1)E\_3 is ACTUAL effective Cartier, full exceptional support, and O(-E) relatively ample. Stacks37.53.6 gives H\textasciicircum{}0(Y,O)=R. The finite affine-section localization/projective immersion/Rees argument proved in n1 applies verbatim in this dimension to THIS explicit Y: it requires normal target, ample negative exceptional bundle and projectivity, not surface resolution. A finite set of sections of O(-nE) gives a proper primary J and identifies Y with Bl\_J(R). This is an explicit construction and no cofinality assertion for arbitrary threefold modifications.

The divisorial part of I on Y is F\_I=E\_1+2E\_2+2E\_3. The order at E\_0 is one, realized by z. In the x-chart of the first blowup, with v=y/x and w=z/x, I O=x(w,x). At P the regular local parameters are x,v,w. Thus the residual ideal (w,x) has point order one, so the second exceptional order is two. The y-chart gives the identical calculation at Q. The strict E\_1 order remains one. All ideal generators are global sections of O(-F\_I) by normal height-one intersection, and at the generic point of EACH E\_i at least one realizes the minimum and restricts to a nonzero section, exactly as in the surface competitor proof.

Nevertheless A=-F\_I fails the mandatory contracted-curve positivity test. On E\_1 let H be the pullback of a line in P\textasciicircum{}2 and e\_P,e\_Q its two exceptional curves. Restricting divisors gives E\_1|\_(E\_1)=-H-e\_P-e\_Q, E\_2|\_(E\_1)=e\_P and E\_3|\_(E\_1)=e\_Q: the strict first divisor is pullback(E\_0)-E\_2-E\_3, and O(E\_0)|\_(E\_0)=O\_P2(-1). Hence
O(-F\_I)|\_(E\_1) has class H-e\_P-e\_Q.
Let L be the strict transform of the line z=0 through P and Q. It is a proper integral contracted curve with class H-e\_P-e\_Q. The usual point-blowup chart calculation gives H\textasciicircum{}2=1, H.e\_P=H.e\_Q=e\_P.e\_Q=0 and e\_P\textasciicircum{}2=e\_Q\textasciicircum{}2=-1; these are local normal-bundle degrees, with residue degrees one. Therefore deg\_L O(-F\_I)=1-1-1=-1. Equivalently the nonzero restricted generator z determines an effective divisor whose fixed component is the negative curve L. A nonzero section on the exceptional SURFACE does not imply nonnegative degree on that curve.

On E\_2 and E\_3 the restrictions of -F\_I are O(1), since E\_1 restricts to a hyperplane and each new exceptional normal bundle is O(-1). Nonnegativity there cannot repair the degree -1 on L in E\_1. Thus positivity was checked on an ACTUAL primary model, and a SPECIFIC actual contracted integral curve violates it; no purely numerical abstract matrix was substituted.

The every-support inequality -v(I)\textless{}=f\_(-F\_I) still holds: the ideal-sheaf inclusion I O\_Y subset O(-F\_I) pulls to the valuation ring of Frac(R/p) for each support p, with generic centre outside the exceptional fibre, and regular ratios give the inequality even for killed generators. What fails is the subsequent inference that f\_(-F\_I) is an all-curve-nef geometric-core competitor on Y. In contrast -v(I) has a negative effective relatively ample Cartier presentation on ACTUAL Bl\_I(R). The example therefore pinpoints a false step in the surface strategy while preserving the positivity of genuine primary profiles and existing smooth characteristic-zero CE. It gives no failure of CE in any characteristic and no theorem about arbitrary higher-dimensional model cofinality.

The intersection numbers are proved by the explicit blowup geometry, not by a program. Own finite\_diagnostics.py only evaluates the recorded integer degree expression and divisor coefficients; it is a diagnostic. \subsection*{Expanded threefold degree obstruction}
For $I=(z)+\mathfrak m^2$ on the explicit regular threefold model,
\[
F_I=E_1+2E_2+2E_3,\quad
(-F_I)|_{E_1}=H-e_P-e_Q,\quad L\sim H-e_P-e_Q,
\]
\[
\deg_L\mathcal O_Y(-F_I)=1-1-1=-1.
\]
The ample actual primary presentation uses
$E=aE_1+(a+1)E_2+(a+1)E_3$ for a sufficiently large integer $a$,
proved by relative composition ampleness and the finite section/Rees
argument. The nonzero generic sections on the $E_i$ therefore fail the
all-contracted-curve test on an actual primary model, not an abstract
unrealized divisor.

\begin{abstract}
On the intrinsic centred semivaluation boundary of an excellent local germ,
we define a geometric core by actual negative effective real Cartier divisors
on actual primary blowups, with nonnegative degrees on all contracted integral
curves. We prove its continuous uniform closure and actual decreasing completion
equal the corresponding ideal classes. A fixed primary correction to finite
global sections, corrected Keeler generation on the full closed fibre, and
explicit real ample perturbation prove the comparison without a normality,
smoothness or characteristic restriction. We reconstruct full finite-injection
and normalization/conductor descent, including mixed-zero and bottom patterns,
and recover the full smooth BFJ class on all semivaluations at its exact published
hypotheses. A nonisolated cusp separates the entire finite-real raw cone from its
completion. This is an unverified complete-solution candidate for P12.2;
independent whole-original review remains required.
\end{abstract}

\section*{Main theorem and proof map}
Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be excellent local and replace it by its reduction.
For every proper primary $\mathfrak a$, let $V_{\mathfrak a}$ be the compact
section of centred real semivaluations with $v(\mathfrak a)=1$, retaining every
prime support. Define $Q$ by finite nonnegative real sums of negative
unit-or-primary ideal values, $C$ by uniform closure, and $D$ by actual infima
of nonempty decreasing $C$-nets, with bottom allowed.

On actual primary blowups, take actual negative effective finite real Cartier
presentations having nonnegative degree on every contracted proper integral
curve. Their evaluations define $M_{\rm geom}$ independently of ideal
positivity. Then
\[
 Q\subset M_{\rm geom}\subset C,\qquad
 C_{\rm geom}=C,\qquad D_{\rm geom}=D.
\]
For every reduced finite injection, the pullback image of either completed
class is precisely its full-fibre-constant subclass. Normalization fibres are
singletons at finite conductor value and precisely conductor-quotient fibres
at infinite value. Primary changes obey the exact homogeneous cocycle.
At a smooth affine closed germ over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero
field, $C$ is exactly the finite continuous BFJ subclass and $D$ is the full
BFJ all-semivaluation extension with the identically bottom function adjoined.
Smooth DVR and empty dimension zero are direct.

The proofs are the numbered local chain below: n1 gives the boundary; n2--n6
give full algebraic descent; n7 discharges the exact published geometric inputs;
n8 constructs and compares the independent cone; n9 gives full BFJ recovery;
n10--n11 preserve raw-cone and radial distinctions; n12 records the exact
original-target decision. Each support list contains only earlier local IDs.
Source applications are ordinary published applications, with disclosed
recursive historical dependencies, and no supplied draft is an accepted premise.
Separate envelopes, products, energy and their geometric operator theory are
the P12.3--P12.6 targets. They are not claimed here.
\section{n1. Boundary, finite lifting, conductor fibres, and completion}
\textit{Earlier local supports: none.}

All rings below are commutative. For a Noetherian local ring \((R,\mathfrak m)\), a centred real semivaluation is a map to \([0,+\infty]\) satisfying \(v(0)=+\infty\), \(v(1)=0\), multiplicativity, the ultrametric inequality, and \(0<v(\mathfrak m)<+\infty\). Its support \(v^{-1}(+\infty)\) is a prime strictly below \(\mathfrak m\); no minimal-support restriction is imposed. Ideal values are minima over finite generating sets. Relations between generating sets prove independence and
\[
v(IJ)=v(I)+v(J),\qquad v(I+J)=\min\{v(I),v(J)\}.
\]
Units have value zero. A proper primary ideal always means primary for the marked maximal ideal.

Fix a proper primary ideal \(\mathfrak a\). The section \(V_{\mathfrak a}(R)=\{v:v(\mathfrak a)=1\}\) is compact Hausdorff. Indeed semivaluation axioms and \(v(\mathfrak m)=1\) are closed conditions in the compact product \([0,+\infty]^R\); addition, minimum and the order relation have the required continuity/closedness. Choose \(\mathfrak m^r\subset\mathfrak a\subset\mathfrak m^s\), with \(r\ge s\ge1\). Then \(s v(\mathfrak m)\le v(\mathfrak a)\le r v(\mathfrak m)\). Rescaling identifies the two sections. Every primary ideal value is finite positive continuous and bounded there. For another primary ideal \(\mathfrak b\),
\[
H_{\mathfrak a,\mathfrak b}(v)=v/v(\mathfrak b),\qquad
u_{\mathfrak b}(H_{\mathfrak a,\mathfrak b}v)=u_{\mathfrak a}(v)/v(\mathfrak b).
\]
The denominator and its inverse are bounded. Substitution proves the inverse and the exact three-gauge cocycle. The centred cone is the product of its section and the positive radial coordinate \(v(\mathfrak a)\); hence these sections represent its intrinsic ray quotient. Nonprimary proper ideals give only their open finite-value domain, which can be noncompact. Units and zero ideals have no level-one section.

Nilpotents have infinite value. Comparing \(f+n\) and \((f+n)-n\) proves that reduction preserves every semivaluation and ideal gauge. A zero-dimensional reduced local ring is a field and has empty centred boundary. For a positive-dimensional excellent reduced local ring, a penultimate prime of a saturated chain to the maximal ideal gives a one-dimensional excellent domain quotient. Its finite normalization has a local DVR factor, whose order pulls back and normalizes into the section. This proves nonemptiness. Ordinary background used throughout is lying over, prime avoidance, finite Artinian decompositions, and the quotient/finite-type-localization stability, Nagata finiteness and universal catenarity consequences of excellence. None is used to assert resolution in arbitrary characteristic.

Every finite integral injection \(R\hookrightarrow B\), with \(B\) reduced and nonzero, gives a continuous surjective restriction \(V_{\mathfrak aB}(B)\to V_{\mathfrak a}(R)\). Here a finite algebra has the finite union of its closed-centre sections above \(\mathfrak m\). To lift \(v\) with support \(\mathfrak p\), choose a prime \(P\) of \(B\) above \(\mathfrak p\) and form the finite field extension \(\operatorname{Frac}(B/P)/\operatorname{Frac}(R/\mathfrak p)\). Extend the valuation ring by taking the integral closure, a prime over its maximal ideal, and a dominating valuation ring. The domination assertion follows from Zorn: in a maximal dominating local subring, either adjoining \(x\) or \(x^{-1}\) leaves the maximal ideal proper. If \(1=\sum a_i x^{-i}\) with all \(a_i\) in that ideal, multiplying by a power and dividing by \(1-a_0\) makes \(x\) integral, so lying over makes the ideal proper after adjoining \(x\). Localizing contradicts maximality unless one of the two elements already belongs. Intersection with the old field is the original valuation ring: an element outside it has inverse in its maximal ideal. An algebraic equation for any new nonzero field element has two least-valued terms; thus an integer multiple of its value is in the old value group. Dividing this old real value by the integer gives a well-defined order-preserving real embedding, using common multiples. Integral elements have nonnegative value by the least-term test for a monic equation. Compose with \(B\to B/P\), assigning \(P\) infinite value. This construction first kills the support; it never extends a nonzero-support semivaluation to the original fraction field. Compactness makes restriction a quotient map.

Let \(S\) be the finite normalization of an excellent reduced \(R\), and \(\mathfrak c=(R:S)\). Clearing denominators of finitely many generators of \(S\) gives a regular conductor element. The conductor is an ideal of both rings and
\[
R=S\times_{S/\mathfrak c}(R/\mathfrak c).
\]
An element of \(S\) whose residue comes from \(R\) differs from an element of \(R\) by an element of \(\mathfrak c\), proving this identity. The normalization boundary is the finite disjoint union of local normal centres above \(\mathfrak m\). Idempotents choose a minimal component; localization at its centre changes no values of denominators. Chinese remainder elements separating the finitely many centres, and their complements, have open positive-value loci selecting these pieces. The pieces are therefore clopen. Branches mean these local centres, not merely minimal primes.

If \(v(\mathfrak c)<+\infty\), choose \(c\in\mathfrak c\) of finite value. Every lift satisfies \(w(s)=v(cs)-v(c)\), so it is unique. The subtracted number is finite. If \(v(\mathfrak c)=+\infty\), all lifts kill the conductor, and equality after restriction \(S/\mathfrak c\to R/\mathfrak c\) is exactly their identification. This infinity stratum is closed; its finite complement is open, without a closedness claim. A fibre-constant continuous function descends continuously by the compact quotient. A fibre-constant extended nonpositive upper semicontinuous function descends upper semicontinuously: its closed superlevel sets have compact, hence closed, images. These assertions alone supply no positivity descent.

Completion preserves the entire boundary for every Noetherian local ring. For \(F\in\widehat R\), choose representatives \(F_N\) modulo \(\mathfrak m^N\), and put \(d=v(\mathfrak m)>0\). Differences have value at least \(Nd\). If some \(v(F_N)<Nd\), later values stabilize; otherwise they tend to infinity. Define \(\widehat v(F)\) by this limit. It is independent of representatives and satisfies
\[
\min\{\widehat v(F),Nd\}=\min\{v(F_N),Nd\}.
\]
Applying this to sums and products proves all axioms and uniqueness, including infinite values; completed units have unit representatives. Compose with \(q(t)=t/(1+t)\), \(q(+\infty)=1\). The displayed continuous truncations have uniform error at most \((1+N/r)^{-1}\), because \(d\ge1/r\). Thus completed evaluation is continuous and completion restriction is a homeomorphism. Every completed primary \(J\) contains \(\widehat{\mathfrak m}^N\). Representatives modulo that power of its generators belong to \(J\cap R\) and, with the boundary power, generate \(J\). Hence \(J=(J\cap R)\widehat R\). The quotient isomorphism also proves contraction of an extended primary ideal recovers it. Unit-or-primary profiles and their uniform and decreasing classes therefore agree under completion. No equivalence of geometric model categories is asserted.

This is a mathematical argument with the stated ordinary background. Swanson--Huneke 6.4.1--2 were read in full and agree with the domination proof. Artifact n1.md is authoritative and is reproduced in argument.md and the paper. Rerun: python audit\_inputs.py; python build\_delivery.py. Execution does not verify the mathematics.

\section{n2. Exact algebraic applications and their qualifications}
\textit{Earlier local supports: n1.}

Use n1 for boundary conventions. The following are ordinary published theorem applications at their stated hypotheses, with their immediate units checked from the bound primary data. This is not an independent reconstruction of all historical proofs.

Swanson--Huneke is the current author PDF created July 2024, not asserted byte-identical to the 2006 printing. Its complete 6.8.1--4 statements and proofs give the following consequence. Let \(E\) be Noetherian semilocal, \(J\) contain a boundary power, and \(Q\) be a finite ideal. If every normalized centred semivaluation satisfies \(w(Q)\ge w(J)\), and \(Q\) has zero image on every zero-dimensional minimal component where \(J\) is nonunit, then
\[
Q\subset\overline J.
\]
Apply 6.8.4 on each positive-dimensional local domain component, normalizing each centred rank-one DVR by the boundary ideal. Apply 6.8.3 to all minimal components. On zero-dimensional components there are no boundary points; the extra zero-image condition is needed. In all uses below \(Q=K(\mathfrak aE)^L\), with \(L>0\), so this condition holds. Unit components of \(J\) impose no condition. For nonreduced \(E\), lift the componentwise monic equations, multiply them over the finitely many minimal primes, then raise to a power to kill the nilpotent error. The resulting equation is monic with degree-i coefficients in \(J^i\). This is the finite-minimal-prime argument of 1.1.5, also read completely. PDF inspection of printed page 140 confirmed the integral-closure bars omitted in the text extraction.

The common exponent needed later has a short direct proof. Each \(qt\), \(q\in Q\), is integral over \(E[Jt]\) with its homogeneous ideal-integrality equation. Thus \(E[(J+Q)t]\) is finite graded over \(E[Jt]\). Choose homogeneous module generators of degrees at most \(h\). Its degree \(N+h\) terms have coefficients in \(J^{N+h-d}\subset J^N\). Consequently
\[
Q^{N+h}\subset (J+Q)^{N+h}\subset J^N\quad(N\ge1).
\]
This works for nonreduced rings and assumes no finiteness of the full normalized Rees algebra. The complete 1.2.3--5 reduction chain was checked too; printed page 6 confirms the criterion uses integral closure.

For an excellent local domain \(A\) of positive dimension and a nonzero proper primary ideal, 10.2.2, including its proof ending at 10.2.3, constructs finitely many Rees DVRs centred at the closed point. The full 10.4.3 proof gives their residue transcendence degree \(\dim A-1\) when \(A\) is formally equidimensional. Excellence supplies universal catenarity, and the full B.5.1 statement/proof identifies this with local formal equidimensionality for Noetherian domains. B.3.2 and its immediate context give, for a finite-type birational domain \(B\) over excellent local domain \(A\), and \(P\cap A=\mathfrak m\),
\[
\dim B_P+\operatorname{trdeg}_{A/\mathfrak m}\kappa(P)=\dim A.
\]
The book describes B.3.2's proof as a modification of an earlier dimension argument. Its recursive historical dependencies, including Mori--Nagata and Ratliff, are disclosed rather than claimed independently read.

Stacks 0C23, full immediate HTML lemma/proof, states that normal Noetherian local rings with normal formal fibres have normal completion; excellence suffices. Its proof refers to Algebra 10.163.8, not recursively read. A normal local completed ring is a domain. Thus an excellent normal local domain is analytically irreducible and analytically unramified. This is not claimed for an arbitrary nonnormal domain.

Exact Rond--Spivakovsky arXiv 1404.4885v1, complete pages 1--2 definitions and statements 1.3, 1.4 and 1.7, yields an order bound at precisely this analytic domain. For a centred divisorial valuation \(\nu\) and the finitely many maximal-ideal Rees valuations \(\nu_j\), 1.3 gives \(\nu(f)\le c_j\nu_j(f)\). Set \(C_0=\max_j c_j\nu_j(\mathfrak m)\). Statements 1.4 and 1.7 imply
\[
\nu(f)\le C_0\min_j\frac{\nu_j(f)}{\nu_j(\mathfrak m)}
=C_0\overline{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak m}(f)
\le C_0(\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak m}(f)+K).
\]
For nonzero nonunits the order is at least one and finite by Krull intersection, giving \(\nu(f)\le C_0(1+K)\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak m}(f)\). Units have both values zero and are handled separately. The literal strict Remark 1.6 fails on units and is not used. Swanson's full Rees-valuations Section 5 was read; its 5.2 is stated for complete domains. The general-domain application here uses exact Rond--Spivakovsky 1.3. Original Rees 1989 and the historical Izumi proofs have not been independently read. Published applications with these disclosed dependencies are allowed by the task; metadata and old reading receipts are not grants.
