\subsection*{Expanded scope-sensitive transport identities}
On the published smooth source, using one fixed compact complex,
\[
 W_t\le W\le(1-C/t)W_{t-C},\qquad t=m+C,
 \qquad (1+C/m)W\le W_m\le W.
\]
All-retraction infima give the all-support estimate
$0\le H-h_m\le CM/m$.
The separable coefficient identity carries the actual degree:
\[
 A_m^{R_{k^s}}(\rho^*f)=A_m^R(f)R_{k^s},\qquad
 h_m^{R_{k^s}}=\rho^*h_m^R,\qquad
 p_{R_{k^s}}(\rho^*f)=\rho^*p_R(f).
\]
The Frobenius-root formula instead gives
\[
 w(s)=q^{-1}v(s^q),\qquad v(J_m)=q\,w(B_m),\qquad
 J_m\subset A_{qm}.
\]
It does not assert the same-degree scalar-extension identity.
For a source bound $E/m$ and $k\ge2q$ the target bound is
$0\le p_R-h_k^R\le(2qE+qM_1)/k$.

\section{n3. New CE theorem for every excellent normal local surface, arbitrary residue field and positive or mixed characteristic.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1.}

NEW scoped theorem: every excellent normal local domain R of dimension TWO, in arbitrary equal or mixed characteristic and with arbitrary residue field, has a greatest continuous independently geometric C/D envelope for EVERY finite continuous signed obstacle on every primary section. The proof uses qualified G331 and own n1, and a dimension-specific published surface resolution theorem at its actual hypotheses. It does not assume higher-dimensional resolution, algebraizability, F-finiteness, CM or Q-Gorenstein hypotheses for arbitrary original R. It includes genuinely positive-characteristic and mixed-characteristic regular source CE in dimension two.

Step 1: actual regular primary models, proved rather than presumed cofinal. Given finitely many primary ideals, INCLUDE the gauge ideal a in this list and begin with their product blowup, which dominates their individual actual blowups by G331. Normalize it finitely by excellence. This is a normal projective integral surface, an isomorphism on U=Spec(R) minus the closed point; U is regular since R is normal of dimension two. Its singular locus is finite: excellence makes the regular locus open, normality gives regularity in codimension one, and a Noetherian closed zero-dimensional subset is finite. Every singular local completion is normal by excellence. Thus the exact condition(4) of Stacks54.14.5/Lipman holds. Its complete immediate proof, together with54.14.3--4 in the entire0BGJ unit, was read. It produces a regular resolution by finitely many normalized blowups. In that proof the local centres resolving this surface are its singular closed points, all over the marked point. Consequently the final regular surface Y is projective and unchanged on U. No analogous result in larger dimension is used.

Y carries an actual effective exceptional integral divisor E with O(-E) relatively ample and with positive coefficient on every exceptional component. On the initial normalized primary blowup the pullback tautological primary divisor is effective and its negative is ample, including under finite normalization. Each subsequent normalized blowup has its effective tautological divisor with relatively ample negative. Inductively combine this negative with a sufficiently large positive multiple of the pullback of the preceding negative ample divisor. Stacks29.38.7--8, whose whole immediate proofs were read, give ampleness of this combination. Pullbacks of effective Cartier divisors are effective here because every modification is birational integral and is generically outside their support. The initial primary divisor vanishes on the whole closed fibre; its pullback has positive order at every final exceptional component. Keeping it with positive multiplicity makes E have the asserted full support. One can add another positive multiple of it without spoiling ampleness, since its negative is generated by the original primary ideal. This also proves a nonempty exceptional fibre, even if R is regular, by including a proper primary blowup at the start.

Here is a full primary presentation argument, to ensure positivity takes place on an ACTUAL primary model. By proper birational normal-target descent, H\textasciicircum{}0(Y,O\_Y)=R (Stacks37.53.6, complete proof read); alternatively proper finiteness plus normal integral closure gives the same statement. L=O(-E) is ample on Y because the base is affine. Choose finitely many sections s\_i of powers of L whose nonvanishing loci Y\_(s\_i) are affine and cover Y. Make their degrees equal by powers, say n. Each affine Y\_(s\_i) is of finite type over R; choose finitely many R-algebra generators for its coordinate ring. The ample-section localization identity, proved in Stacks28.27.11, writes each generator as t/s\_i\textasciicircum{}k with t a section of L\textasciicircum{}(nk). Choose a common K for all these exponents; the sections s\_i\textasciicircum{}K and t s\_i\textasciicircum{}(K-k) of L\textasciicircum{}(nK) yield a projective-space map. In the s\_i\textasciicircum{}K chart their ratios contain all chosen affine generators, so Y\_(s\_i) maps as a closed subscheme of that affine chart. These charts cover the image; the map is an immersion. Y is proper over R and projective space is separated, so the immersion is proper and hence a closed immersion.

All the selected sections belong to R because L\textasciicircum{}(nK) subset O\_Y. Let J be their ideal in R. They generate L\textasciicircum{}(nK), so J O\_Y=L\textasciicircum{}(nK). The ideal is proper: a unit would be a section vanishing on the nonempty E. Off the marked point L is canonically O and generation shows J is the unit ideal. Thus J is proper primary. The projective-space map factors through Bl\_J(R), since J O\_Y is invertible. This blowup is the integral scheme Proj(R[Jt]) inside the same projective space. The factor is a closed immersion containing its generic point and indeed its dense U. A closed subscheme of an integral reduced scheme containing a dense open must have zero defining ideal. Hence Y IS Bl\_J(R). This proves precisely the regular primary refinements needed for finitely many ideal functions; no arbitrary-model cofinality is assumed. The finite section-generation/localization units used here were read with their whole immediate proofs.

Step 2: the finite degree problem. Write the exceptional curves of Y as E\_1,...,E\_r. Y is regular, so these integral Weil divisors are actual effective Cartier divisors (regular Noetherian local rings are factorial). No SNC or residue-field perfection is required. Write a O\_Y=O(-E\_a), E\_a=sum a\_i E\_i, a\_i positive integers, and let a signed model obstacle be f\_D, D=sum d\_i E\_i. Every finite real signed sum of primary gauges is of this form on the regular primary refinement above.

Define Q\_ij=deg\_(E\_i) O(E\_j), with degree over the residue field of R. For i!=j this is nonnegative: restriction gives an effective finite zero divisor, and its degree is the sum of local intersection lengths times residue degrees. The same length O\_(Y,x)/(f\_i,f\_j) computes Q\_ji, so Q is symmetric. Nonzero sections of any line bundle on a proper integral curve have nonnegative degree by the effective zero-divisor exact sequence. These arguments apply to nonsmooth integral curves and imperfect residue fields. All contracted proper integral curves on Y are among the E\_i. Thus an exceptional real divisor sum z\_i E\_i is curve-nef exactly when Qz>=0.

Put c\_i=min(d\_i,0). The feasible vectors are z<=c and Qz>=0. They are nonempty: if M is any nonnegative number with f\_D>=-M on X, the divisor -M E\_a is a negative effective nef barrier, since -E\_a is a pullback of the generated primary tautological bundle. Its coefficient vector b\_i=-M a\_i satisfies b<=c. Coefficientwise maximum preserves feasibility: at coordinate i choose whichever input has its ith coordinate equal to the maximum; the diagonal contribution is unchanged and every off-diagonal contribution increases because Q\_ij>=0. The subset b<=z<=c, Qz>=0 is compact. Maximize each of its finitely many coordinates and then take the finite coordinatewise maximum of these maximizers. The result z* is feasible and dominates the whole subset. Any original feasible z has max(z,b) in that subset, so z* is the GREATEST feasible vector of the entire problem.

Z*=sum z\_i* E\_i is an actual negative effective finite REAL Cartier presentation and has nonnegative degree on EVERY contracted integral curve. Y is an ACTUAL primary blowup by Step1. Therefore its evaluation H=f\_(Z*) belongs to G331's independent geometric core, hence continuous C. Coefficientwise Z*<=D implies H<=f\_D on EVERY support by actual Cartier effectivity. In particular this is actual geometric positivity, established before any envelope comparison.

Step 3: every competitor is controlled. Let q=-v(I)/s be a rational primary profile below f\_D, s a positive integer. On Y form F\_I=sum ord\_(E\_i)(I) E\_i, an actual effective Cartier divisor. Every generator of I is a global section of O(-F\_I): its height-one orders have the required lower bounds on the exceptional curves, and are nonnegative at horizontal primes; normal height-one intersection gives regularity after dividing by the Cartier generator. At the generic point of any E\_i, some ideal generator attains ord\_(E\_i)(I). Its resulting section of O(-F\_I)|\_(E\_i) is nonzero. Hence deg\_(E\_i) O(-F\_I)>=0 for EVERY i, by the curve argument above. Consequently A=-F\_I/s has Q-coefficients>=0 and is nonpositive.

The normalized divisorial points v\_i=ord\_(E\_i)/a\_i lie in X. Testing q<=f\_D there gives A\_i<=d\_i, so A is feasible and A<=Z*. Moreover I O\_Y subset O(-F\_I) gives q(v)<=f\_A(v) on every semivaluation, including nonzero support. To see this without an illicit fraction-field extension, pull both sheaves to the valuation ring of Frac(R/p), where p is that support. Properness gives its centre on Y; its generic point lies over U. The exceptional invertible lattice has a nonzero generic trivialization. Its local generator b and every ideal generator g have g/b regular at the centre, so v(g)>=v(b); a primary generator has finite value, and generators killed by p have infinite value and satisfy the same inequality. Thus q<=f\_A<=H EVERYWHERE. The unit case is identical with F\_I=0 when zero is a competitor.

N1 identifies the raw D envelope with the supremum of these rational competitors. It follows p(f\_D)<=H. The reverse follows because H itself is a C competitor. Hence p(f\_D)=H is the greatest C/D minorant, with actual core positivity. This is not norm averaging, a trace of a greatest minorant, or a claim that positivity follows from a raw supremum. No competing-model cofinality or arbitrary nef b-divisor pushforward is needed: the explicit ideal-section argument controls all competitors.

Step 4: all continuous obstacles and regularity. The signed real span of primary profiles is uniformly dense in C(X,R). For clarity the ideal identity proving the lattice condition is min(v(I)-v(J),v(K)-v(H))=v(IH+KJ)-v(JH), after common denominators; the span contains constants through v(a)=1. The truncated profiles v((g)+a\textasciicircum{}N)=min(v(g),N) separate every pair of semivaluations, including different supports. The compact lattice Stone-Weierstrass argument gives density, as also granted and proved in G331. Choose signed model f\_j uniformly converging to f. Their envelopes H\_j above are in C. N1's raw Lipschitz estimate makes H\_j uniformly Cauchy with limit p(f); closedness of C proves p(f) continuous and geometrically positive. Every canonical degree converges uniformly by n1. Exact primary-gauge changes and completion follow n1/G331. For decreasing continuous obstacles the actual D-envelope rule in n1 gives upper semicontinuous completed envelopes, including poles and bottom. No O(1/m) canonical rate or canonical FG for general surface model obstacles is claimed. A rational d yields a rational maximal coefficient vector by finite rational linear programming, but this does not identify its nef line bundle with a single ideal profile or prove semiampleness.

There is an explicit mixed-characteristic and positive-characteristic diagnostic instance. For a two-dimensional excellent regular local ring with parameters x,y and residue field k, blow up m=(x,y) and then the k-rational point y/x=0 of its exceptional curve. The affine point-blowup charts are regular: at a point of the exceptional curve their maximal ideals have the expected two generators and dimension two. The strict first curve E\_1 and second curve E\_2 have degree matrix Q=[[-2,1],[1,-1]]: the first self-degree decreases by one from -1, the new exceptional has degree -1, and their transverse intersection has length one. Pullback E\_m=E\_1+E\_2. The negative of 2E\_1+3E\_2 has strictly positive degrees on both curves. Stacks33.44.15 on the full closed fibre and30.21.4 over the local base, both with complete immediate proofs read, make it relatively ample. The primary presentation argument in Step1 then realizes this very two-blowup surface as an actual primary model after an ample power. For obstacle D=-E\_1-3E\_2, the greatest feasible vector is (-3/2,-3), with Qz*=(0,3/2). The ideal I=(x\textasciicircum{}2,y) becomes generated with divisor E\_1+2E\_2 on these two blowups: in the x chart it is x(x,y/x), whose second blowup principalizes the residual maximal ideal; at the other centres it is already invertible. Therefore the exact envelope is -3v(I)/2. This example is valid for k[[x,y]] in every characteristic and for Z\_p[[u]] with x=p,y=u; the latter is a complete excellent regular surface of mixed characteristic. \subsection*{Expanded finite surface degree construction}
On the regular ACTUAL primary model, write
\[
 E_{\mathfrak a}=\sum_{i=1}^r a_i E_i,\qquad a_i>0,
 \qquad D=\sum_i d_i E_i,\qquad
 Q_{ij}=\deg_{E_i}\mathcal O_Y(E_j).
\]
For $i\ne j$, $Q_{ij}\ge0$ is the symmetric local intersection-length
sum. Every contracted integral curve is an $E_i$. If
$b_i=-Ma_i$ is the primary nef barrier and $c_i=\min(d_i,0)$, set
\[
 K=\{z\in\mathbb R^r:b\le z\le c,\ Qz\ge0\}.
\]
It is nonempty compact. For each $i$ choose a coordinate maximizer
$z^{(i)}\in K$. If $u=\max(z,w)$, choose $z_i=u_i$; then
\[
 (Qu)_i=Q_{ii}z_i+\sum_{j\ne i}Q_{ij}u_j
 \ge (Qz)_i\ge0.
\]
Thus $z^*=\max_i z^{(i)}\in K$ is its greatest vector. Replacing any
unbounded feasible vector $z$ by $\max(z,b)$ proves it is greatest
in the full problem as well. The actual negative effective divisor
$Z^*=\sum_i z_i^*E_i$ is curve-nef before the envelope is identified.

For every rational competitor $q_I=-v(I)/s$, put
\[
 F_I=\sum_i\operatorname{ord}_{E_i}(I)E_i,\qquad A=-F_I/s.
\]
The inclusion $I\mathcal O_Y\subset\mathcal O_Y(-F_I)$ and a generator
attaining the generic minimum on each $E_i$ give a nonzero section of
$\mathcal O_Y(-F_I)|_{E_i}$. Its degree is nonnegative. Hence $QA\ge0$.
Testing $q_I\le f_D$ at $\operatorname{ord}_{E_i}/a_i$ yields
$A_i\le d_i$, and the valued quotient-field lattice proves
\[
 q_I(v)\le f_A(v)\le f_{Z^*}(v)\qquad\text{for EVERY support}.
\]
The rational-competitor identity in n1 therefore gives
$p(f_D)=f_{Z^*}\in C_{\rm geom}$. Uniform signed-model density and
$\|p(f)-p(g)\|\le\|f-g\|$ give the theorem for every continuous signed
obstacle. There is no claimed canonical approximation rate.

The two-blowup computation is
\[
 Q=\begin{pmatrix}-2&1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad
 d=\binom{-1}{-3},\quad z^*=\binom{-3/2}{-3},\quad
 Qz^*=\binom0{3/2}.
\]
With $I=(x^2,y)$ and $F_I=E_1+2E_2$, the actual envelope is
$p(f_D)=-\tfrac32 v(I)$, also on $\mathbb Z_p[[u]]$ for $x=p,y=u$.

\begin{abstract}
On the intrinsic centred semivaluation boundary of an excellent local germ,
we define a geometric core by actual negative effective real Cartier divisors
on actual primary blowups, with nonnegative degrees on all contracted integral
curves. We prove its continuous uniform closure and actual decreasing completion
equal the corresponding ideal classes. A fixed primary correction to finite
global sections, corrected Keeler generation on the full closed fibre, and
explicit real ample perturbation prove the comparison without a normality,
smoothness or characteristic restriction. We reconstruct full finite-injection
and normalization/conductor descent, including mixed-zero and bottom patterns,
and recover the full smooth BFJ class on all semivaluations at its exact published
hypotheses. A nonisolated cusp separates the entire finite-real raw cone from its
completion. This is an unverified complete-solution candidate for P12.2;
independent whole-original review remains required.
\end{abstract}
\tableofcontents
\newpage
\section*{Main theorem and proof map}
Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be excellent local and replace it by its reduction.
For every proper primary $\mathfrak a$, let $V_{\mathfrak a}$ be the compact
section of centred real semivaluations with $v(\mathfrak a)=1$, retaining every
prime support. Define $Q$ by finite nonnegative real sums of negative
unit-or-primary ideal values, $C$ by uniform closure, and $D$ by actual infima
of nonempty decreasing $C$-nets, with bottom allowed.

On actual primary blowups, take actual negative effective finite real Cartier
presentations having nonnegative degree on every contracted proper integral
curve. Their evaluations define $M_{\rm geom}$ independently of ideal
positivity. Then
\[
 Q\subset M_{\rm geom}\subset C,\qquad
 C_{\rm geom}=C,\qquad D_{\rm geom}=D.
\]
For every reduced finite injection, the pullback image of either completed
class is precisely its full-fibre-constant subclass. Normalization fibres are
singletons at finite conductor value and precisely conductor-quotient fibres
at infinite value. Primary changes obey the exact homogeneous cocycle.
At a smooth affine closed germ over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero
field, $C$ is exactly the finite continuous BFJ subclass and $D$ is the full
BFJ all-semivaluation extension with the identically bottom function adjoined.
Smooth DVR and empty dimension zero are direct.

The proofs are the numbered local chain below: n1 gives the boundary; n2--n6
give full algebraic descent; n7 discharges the exact published geometric inputs;
n8 constructs and compares the independent cone; n9 gives full BFJ recovery;
n10--n11 preserve raw-cone and radial distinctions; n12 records the exact
original-target decision. Each support list contains only earlier local IDs.
Source applications are ordinary published applications, with disclosed
recursive historical dependencies, and no supplied draft is an accepted premise.
Separate envelopes, products, energy and their geometric operator theory are
the P12.3--P12.6 targets. They are not claimed here.
\section{n1. Boundary, finite lifting, conductor fibres, and completion}
\textit{Earlier local supports: none.}

All rings below are commutative. For a Noetherian local ring \((R,\mathfrak m)\), a centred real semivaluation is a map to \([0,+\infty]\) satisfying \(v(0)=+\infty\), \(v(1)=0\), multiplicativity, the ultrametric inequality, and \(0<v(\mathfrak m)<+\infty\). Its support \(v^{-1}(+\infty)\) is a prime strictly below \(\mathfrak m\); no minimal-support restriction is imposed. Ideal values are minima over finite generating sets. Relations between generating sets prove independence and
\[
v(IJ)=v(I)+v(J),\qquad v(I+J)=\min\{v(I),v(J)\}.
\]
Units have value zero. A proper primary ideal always means primary for the marked maximal ideal.

Fix a proper primary ideal \(\mathfrak a\). The section \(V_{\mathfrak a}(R)=\{v:v(\mathfrak a)=1\}\) is compact Hausdorff. Indeed semivaluation axioms and \(v(\mathfrak m)=1\) are closed conditions in the compact product \([0,+\infty]^R\); addition, minimum and the order relation have the required continuity/closedness. Choose \(\mathfrak m^r\subset\mathfrak a\subset\mathfrak m^s\), with \(r\ge s\ge1\). Then \(s v(\mathfrak m)\le v(\mathfrak a)\le r v(\mathfrak m)\). Rescaling identifies the two sections. Every primary ideal value is finite positive continuous and bounded there. For another primary ideal \(\mathfrak b\),
\[
H_{\mathfrak a,\mathfrak b}(v)=v/v(\mathfrak b),\qquad
u_{\mathfrak b}(H_{\mathfrak a,\mathfrak b}v)=u_{\mathfrak a}(v)/v(\mathfrak b).
\]
The denominator and its inverse are bounded. Substitution proves the inverse and the exact three-gauge cocycle. The centred cone is the product of its section and the positive radial coordinate \(v(\mathfrak a)\); hence these sections represent its intrinsic ray quotient. Nonprimary proper ideals give only their open finite-value domain, which can be noncompact. Units and zero ideals have no level-one section.

Nilpotents have infinite value. Comparing \(f+n\) and \((f+n)-n\) proves that reduction preserves every semivaluation and ideal gauge. A zero-dimensional reduced local ring is a field and has empty centred boundary. For a positive-dimensional excellent reduced local ring, a penultimate prime of a saturated chain to the maximal ideal gives a one-dimensional excellent domain quotient. Its finite normalization has a local DVR factor, whose order pulls back and normalizes into the section. This proves nonemptiness. Ordinary background used throughout is lying over, prime avoidance, finite Artinian decompositions, and the quotient/finite-type-localization stability, Nagata finiteness and universal catenarity consequences of excellence. None is used to assert resolution in arbitrary characteristic.

Every finite integral injection \(R\hookrightarrow B\), with \(B\) reduced and nonzero, gives a continuous surjective restriction \(V_{\mathfrak aB}(B)\to V_{\mathfrak a}(R)\). Here a finite algebra has the finite union of its closed-centre sections above \(\mathfrak m\). To lift \(v\) with support \(\mathfrak p\), choose a prime \(P\) of \(B\) above \(\mathfrak p\) and form the finite field extension \(\operatorname{Frac}(B/P)/\operatorname{Frac}(R/\mathfrak p)\). Extend the valuation ring by taking the integral closure, a prime over its maximal ideal, and a dominating valuation ring. The domination assertion follows from Zorn: in a maximal dominating local subring, either adjoining \(x\) or \(x^{-1}\) leaves the maximal ideal proper. If \(1=\sum a_i x^{-i}\) with all \(a_i\) in that ideal, multiplying by a power and dividing by \(1-a_0\) makes \(x\) integral, so lying over makes the ideal proper after adjoining \(x\). Localizing contradicts maximality unless one of the two elements already belongs. Intersection with the old field is the original valuation ring: an element outside it has inverse in its maximal ideal. An algebraic equation for any new nonzero field element has two least-valued terms; thus an integer multiple of its value is in the old value group. Dividing this old real value by the integer gives a well-defined order-preserving real embedding, using common multiples. Integral elements have nonnegative value by the least-term test for a monic equation. Compose with \(B\to B/P\), assigning \(P\) infinite value. This construction first kills the support; it never extends a nonzero-support semivaluation to the original fraction field. Compactness makes restriction a quotient map.

Let \(S\) be the finite normalization of an excellent reduced \(R\), and \(\mathfrak c=(R:S)\). Clearing denominators of finitely many generators of \(S\) gives a regular conductor element. The conductor is an ideal of both rings and
\[
R=S\times_{S/\mathfrak c}(R/\mathfrak c).
\]
An element of \(S\) whose residue comes from \(R\) differs from an element of \(R\) by an element of \(\mathfrak c\), proving this identity. The normalization boundary is the finite disjoint union of local normal centres above \(\mathfrak m\). Idempotents choose a minimal component; localization at its centre changes no values of denominators. Chinese remainder elements separating the finitely many centres, and their complements, have open positive-value loci selecting these pieces. The pieces are therefore clopen. Branches mean these local centres, not merely minimal primes.

If \(v(\mathfrak c)<+\infty\), choose \(c\in\mathfrak c\) of finite value. Every lift satisfies \(w(s)=v(cs)-v(c)\), so it is unique. The subtracted number is finite. If \(v(\mathfrak c)=+\infty\), all lifts kill the conductor, and equality after restriction \(S/\mathfrak c\to R/\mathfrak c\) is exactly their identification. This infinity stratum is closed; its finite complement is open, without a closedness claim. A fibre-constant continuous function descends continuously by the compact quotient. A fibre-constant extended nonpositive upper semicontinuous function descends upper semicontinuously: its closed superlevel sets have compact, hence closed, images. These assertions alone supply no positivity descent.

Completion preserves the entire boundary for every Noetherian local ring. For \(F\in\widehat R\), choose representatives \(F_N\) modulo \(\mathfrak m^N\), and put \(d=v(\mathfrak m)>0\). Differences have value at least \(Nd\). If some \(v(F_N)<Nd\), later values stabilize; otherwise they tend to infinity. Define \(\widehat v(F)\) by this limit. It is independent of representatives and satisfies
\[
\min\{\widehat v(F),Nd\}=\min\{v(F_N),Nd\}.
\]
Applying this to sums and products proves all axioms and uniqueness, including infinite values; completed units have unit representatives. Compose with \(q(t)=t/(1+t)\), \(q(+\infty)=1\). The displayed continuous truncations have uniform error at most \((1+N/r)^{-1}\), because \(d\ge1/r\). Thus completed evaluation is continuous and completion restriction is a homeomorphism. Every completed primary \(J\) contains \(\widehat{\mathfrak m}^N\). Representatives modulo that power of its generators belong to \(J\cap R\) and, with the boundary power, generate \(J\). Hence \(J=(J\cap R)\widehat R\). The quotient isomorphism also proves contraction of an extended primary ideal recovers it. Unit-or-primary profiles and their uniform and decreasing classes therefore agree under completion. No equivalence of geometric model categories is asserted.

This is a mathematical argument with the stated ordinary background. Swanson--Huneke 6.4.1--2 were read in full and agree with the domination proof. Artifact n1.md is authoritative and is reproduced in argument.md and the paper. Rerun: python audit\_inputs.py; python build\_delivery.py. Execution does not verify the mathematics.

\section{n2. Exact algebraic applications and their qualifications}
\textit{Earlier local supports: n1.}

Use n1 for boundary conventions. The following are ordinary published theorem applications at their stated hypotheses, with their immediate units checked from the bound primary data. This is not an independent reconstruction of all historical proofs.

Swanson--Huneke is the current author PDF created July 2024, not asserted byte-identical to the 2006 printing. Its complete 6.8.1--4 statements and proofs give the following consequence. Let \(E\) be Noetherian semilocal, \(J\) contain a boundary power, and \(Q\) be a finite ideal. If every normalized centred semivaluation satisfies \(w(Q)\ge w(J)\), and \(Q\) has zero image on every zero-dimensional minimal component where \(J\) is nonunit, then
\[
Q\subset\overline J.
\]
Apply 6.8.4 on each positive-dimensional local domain component, normalizing each centred rank-one DVR by the boundary ideal. Apply 6.8.3 to all minimal components. On zero-dimensional components there are no boundary points; the extra zero-image condition is needed. In all uses below \(Q=K(\mathfrak aE)^L\), with \(L>0\), so this condition holds. Unit components of \(J\) impose no condition. For nonreduced \(E\), lift the componentwise monic equations, multiply them over the finitely many minimal primes, then raise to a power to kill the nilpotent error. The resulting equation is monic with degree-i coefficients in \(J^i\). This is the finite-minimal-prime argument of 1.1.5, also read completely. PDF inspection of printed page 140 confirmed the integral-closure bars omitted in the text extraction.

The common exponent needed later has a short direct proof. Each \(qt\), \(q\in Q\), is integral over \(E[Jt]\) with its homogeneous ideal-integrality equation. Thus \(E[(J+Q)t]\) is finite graded over \(E[Jt]\). Choose homogeneous module generators of degrees at most \(h\). Its degree \(N+h\) terms have coefficients in \(J^{N+h-d}\subset J^N\). Consequently
\[
Q^{N+h}\subset (J+Q)^{N+h}\subset J^N\quad(N\ge1).
\]
This works for nonreduced rings and assumes no finiteness of the full normalized Rees algebra. The complete 1.2.3--5 reduction chain was checked too; printed page 6 confirms the criterion uses integral closure.

For an excellent local domain \(A\) of positive dimension and a nonzero proper primary ideal, 10.2.2, including its proof ending at 10.2.3, constructs finitely many Rees DVRs centred at the closed point. The full 10.4.3 proof gives their residue transcendence degree \(\dim A-1\) when \(A\) is formally equidimensional. Excellence supplies universal catenarity, and the full B.5.1 statement/proof identifies this with local formal equidimensionality for Noetherian domains. B.3.2 and its immediate context give, for a finite-type birational domain \(B\) over excellent local domain \(A\), and \(P\cap A=\mathfrak m\),
\[
\dim B_P+\operatorname{trdeg}_{A/\mathfrak m}\kappa(P)=\dim A.
\]
The book describes B.3.2's proof as a modification of an earlier dimension argument. Its recursive historical dependencies, including Mori--Nagata and Ratliff, are disclosed rather than claimed independently read.

Stacks 0C23, full immediate HTML lemma/proof, states that normal Noetherian local rings with normal formal fibres have normal completion; excellence suffices. Its proof refers to Algebra 10.163.8, not recursively read. A normal local completed ring is a domain. Thus an excellent normal local domain is analytically irreducible and analytically unramified. This is not claimed for an arbitrary nonnormal domain.

Exact Rond--Spivakovsky arXiv 1404.4885v1, complete pages 1--2 definitions and statements 1.3, 1.4 and 1.7, yields an order bound at precisely this analytic domain. For a centred divisorial valuation \(\nu\) and the finitely many maximal-ideal Rees valuations \(\nu_j\), 1.3 gives \(\nu(f)\le c_j\nu_j(f)\). Set \(C_0=\max_j c_j\nu_j(\mathfrak m)\). Statements 1.4 and 1.7 imply
\[
\nu(f)\le C_0\min_j\frac{\nu_j(f)}{\nu_j(\mathfrak m)}
=C_0\overline{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak m}(f)
\le C_0(\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak m}(f)+K).
\]
For nonzero nonunits the order is at least one and finite by Krull intersection, giving \(\nu(f)\le C_0(1+K)\operatorname{ord}_{\mathfrak m}(f)\). Units have both values zero and are handled separately. The literal strict Remark 1.6 fails on units and is not used. Swanson's full Rees-valuations Section 5 was read; its 5.2 is stated for complete domains. The general-domain application here uses exact Rond--Spivakovsky 1.3. Original Rees 1989 and the historical Izumi proofs have not been independently read. Published applications with these disclosed dependencies are allowed by the task; metadata and old reading receipts are not grants.
