\section{n3. New exact separable coefficient-field descent gives all continuous envelopes on finite smooth quotients over any characteristic-zero field.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1, n2.}

NEW algebraic-field descent, proved independently. Let k be any field, let (R,m) be a positive-dimensional normal domain essentially of finite type over k with residue field EXACTLY k, and assume geometric integrality and geometric normality. Fix primary a. Let k\textasciicircum{}s be a separable closure and put R\_s=R tensor\_k k\textasciicircum{}s. It is the local ring at the corresponding rational point: for every finite Galois E/k, R\_E=R tensor\_k E is finite free and has a unique maximal ideal, since R\_E/mR\_E=E and every maximal ideal in a finite integral algebra lies over m. The directed union is local, and is the corresponding finite-type localized k\textasciicircum{}s algebra, hence Noetherian excellent normal. Geometric hypotheses are explicit; no assertion about arbitrary excellent germs or completions is made. All semivaluation supports are retained.

Write X\_E=V\_(aR\_E)(R\_E), X\_s=V\_(aR\_s)(R\_s), and Gamma=Gal(k\textasciicircum{}s/k). G331 supplies surjective restriction at every finite stage. There is an actual homeomorphism X\_s=lim\_E X\_E: a compatible tuple defines a semivaluation on the union, satisfies the axioms and a-normalization there, and conversely every semivaluation restricts. Coordinates belong to a finite stage, so the topology is exactly the inverse-limit topology. Compactness and finite-stage surjectivity prove rho:X\_s-\textgreater{}X=V\_a(R) is continuous surjective, also at nonminimal supports. One can see surjectivity by the finite-intersection property for the nonempty compact finite-stage fibres.

Every rho-fibre is a FULL Gamma orbit. Indeed R\_E\textasciicircum{}Gal(E/k)=R: expand in a k-basis of E and impose invariance, or use the trace-dual coefficient argument below. Its generic extension is Galois of degree [E:k] by geometric integrality and n2's fixed-field proof. Thus G331's full-support normal-target coefficient trace identifies each finite-stage fibre with a full Gal(E/k) orbit. Given w,w' in the same infinite fibre, let C\_E be the set of gamma in Gamma for which their E-restrictions agree after gamma. Each C\_E is nonempty clopen, because finite-field automorphisms extend to k\textasciicircum{}s and the fibre is an orbit. Finite intersections contain C\_F for a common finite Galois F. The profinite compactness of Gamma gives a common gamma. This proves full transitivity, not merely transitivity on original-field valuations. Gamma is the inverse limit of the finite Galois groups; its compactness and restriction surjectivity are ordinary separable field theory, and the finite-intersection argument is supplied here.

Here is COMPLETE ideal descent, avoiding an infinite average. For finite Galois E/k choose a k-basis e\_j and trace-dual c\_i with Tr(c\_i e\_j)=delta\_ij. Nondegeneracy can be proved without an extra theorem: n2's evaluation-matrix fixed-field proof gives a basis whose matrix M=(sigma(e\_j)) is invertible. The trace Gram matrix is M\textasciicircum{}t M, with determinant (det M)\textasciicircum{}2 nonzero; its entries lie in k. Thus a trace-dual basis exists, also when the characteristic divides the group order. If an ideal I\_E in R\_E is Galois-stable and s=sum\_j r\_j e\_j belongs to it, then sum\_sigma sigma(c\_i s)=r\_i belongs to I\_E intersect R. Hence I\_E=(I\_E intersect R)R\_E.

If an ideal I\_s in R\_s is Gamma-stable, choose a finite Galois E containing its finite set of generators and let I\_E be the generated ideal. R\_s is faithfully flat over R\_E (field extension), so I\_s intersect R\_E=I\_E. Every automorphism of E extends to Gamma, and stability of I\_s forces stability of I\_E. Finite descent therefore gives I\_s=(I\_s intersect R)R\_s. This proves the assertion on the entire Noetherian algebra, not just on a generic field. Primary and unit ideals retain their type: proper primary ideals contain a power of m\_s=mR\_s, and faithful contraction contains a power of m and remains proper.

For any finite continuous signed f on X, put F=f composed with rho. The source canonical B\_m=A\_m\textasciicircum{}(R\_s)(F) is Gamma-stable by its all-support definition. Restriction surjectivity proves B\_m intersect R=A\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f). Ideal descent now gives the exact EVERY-degree identity

 B\_m=A\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f)R\_s,  h\_m\textasciicircum{}(R\_s)=rho\textasciicircum{}*h\_m\textasciicircum{}R,
 direct-sum B\_m t\textasciicircum{}m=(direct-sum A\_m\textasciicircum{}R t\textasciicircum{}m) tensor\_k k\textasciicircum{}s.

Taking the raw supremum identity of n1 proves rho\textasciicircum{}*p\_R(f)=p\_(R\_s)(F), WITHOUT first assuming either envelope continuous. Continuity of either raw envelope is equivalent to continuity of the other: pullback preserves continuity, and continuous fibre-constant functions descend through the compact quotient rho. The uniform criterion of n1 then proves positivity in C whenever continuous. Sup norms and all uniform canonical error bounds are preserved exactly, without a field-degree loss. Finite generation of the two canonical algebras is also equivalent: if the source is generated in degrees \textless{}=D, the multiplication maps from base degrees \textless{}=D become surjective after faithfully flat extension, hence were already surjective; finitely many generators of those Noetherian ideals suffice. The reverse is base extension. This is an equivalence, not general finite generation.

For completeness the positive continuous cone itself descends: rho\textasciicircum{}*C\_R is precisely the Gamma-invariant subclass of C\_(R\_s). Pullback is an isometry and preserves C,D by primary ideal extension and actual limits. If H in source C is invariant, approximate it uniformly within epsilon by -w(I)/r. Descend its generators and primary-power containment to finite Galois E. Replace I\_E by I'\_E=sum\_sigma sigma(I\_E), whose profile is the maximum of its translates and is within epsilon of H on every source point, by Gamma invariance. I'\_E is stable, unit or proper primary. The trace-dual proof descends it to J in R, giving the approximant -v(J)/r downstairs. Compact quotient descent and these uniform approximants put H in C\_R. No assertion that arbitrary invariant D nets automatically descend through an infinite extension is needed or made.

Actual NEW CE application: in characteristic zero k\textasciicircum{}s is an algebraic closure. Let a finite group G act faithfully by k-linear transformations on k[x\_1,...,x\_n], preserving the origin, and take R=(k[x]\textasciicircum{}G) at its origin. The same result holds for any such geometrically normal rational germ whose algebraic-closure ring has the finite smooth-quotient presentation of n2. In the displayed linear case, invariants commute with field extension: the fixed-subspace equations on each finite-dimensional polynomial degree piece commute with tensoring, and localization commutes by invariant orbit denominator products. The invariant ring is finite type and k[x] finite over it: each variable satisfies its monic orbit polynomial, giving finiteness over the finite coefficient subalgebra; the invariant ring is its Noetherian submodule. All nonleading orbit-polynomial coefficients vanish at the origin, so modulo the invariant origin ideal every variable is nilpotent. Thus the origin is the unique centre above it, and the localized smooth cover is actually finite local. Normality follows from the integral invariant-fraction argument in n2, and the same argument after any field extension gives geometric normality and integrality. Thus R\_s is the exact algebraically closed characteristic-zero finite smooth quotient of n2. That note applies BFJ ONLY there. Our independently proved full-fibre and ideal descent now gives a greatest continuous geometric C/D envelope for EVERY finite continuous signed obstacle on R over ANY characteristic-zero field k.

For each signed REAL Cartier obstacle on an ACTUAL primary model, G331's section formula is a finite signed primary combination. Its scalar pullback is therefore a model obstacle in n2. The n2 quotient O(1/m) full-degree canonical estimate transfers EXACTLY to R through the every-degree identity above. The constant may depend on the geometric source model and obstacle; there is no field-extension-degree factor or finite-generation hypothesis. Exact primary-gauge changes leave all canonical ideals unchanged and divide errors by the positive gauge denominator.

In particular k=Q and R=Q[a,b,c,z\_1,...,z\_r]\_(a,b,c,z)/(ac-b\textasciicircum{}2), r\textgreater{}=1, are actual normal nonisolated germs covered by the theorem. Normality, dimension r+2 and singular locus of dimension r follow from n2's explicit invariant presentation and derivative/embedding-dimension computation, which remain valid over characteristic-zero k. The same nonlinear signed primary obstacle and two cancellation-sensitive valuations in n2 exist over Q. Its continuous geometric envelope now follows over Q itself, rather than by applying a published algebraically closed theorem directly to Q. This supplies a complete new scoped theorem beyond whole410's stated base-field domain. It does not treat arbitrary normal singularities without the geometric smooth-quotient presentation, mixed characteristic, or provide a positive-characteristic CE source theorem. All normalization/conductor rules remain conditional on EVERY actual fibre; products and energy remain separate.

Artifacts: n3.txt, distinct full TeX exposition n3.tex, P12.3-algebraic-descent-envelopes.tex and the entire companion/reading ledger. Rerun: python assemble\_delivery.py; python finite\_checks.py; python check\_delivery.py. The universal arguments are proofs; the trace-dual finite example checks only its stated arithmetic domain.

\subsection*{Expanded field-descent equalities}
The infinite group is used by finite clopen compatibility conditions:
\[
 X_{k^s}=\varprojlim_E X_E,\qquad
 \bigcap_E C_E\ne\varnothing,\qquad
 \rho^{-1}(v)=\Gamma\cdot w.
\]
Trace-dual extraction on a stable ideal is an actual ring identity:
\[
 s=\sum_jr_je_j\in I_E\ \Longrightarrow\
 r_i=\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(E/k)}\sigma(c_is)
 \in I_E\cap R.
\]
It uses separability, not division by the group order. In particular it
also applies when the field characteristic divides that order. It yields
\[
 A_m^{R_{k^s}}(\rho^*f)=A_m^R(f)R_{k^s},\qquad
 h_m^{R_{k^s}}=\rho^*h_m^R,\qquad
 p_{R_{k^s}}(\rho^*f)=\rho^*p_R(f).
\]
Unlike a coefficient norm approximation, this carries the canonical
degree $m$ itself unchanged. The characteristic-zero application is a
descent theorem proved here, after using the published smooth theory
only over the algebraic closure. It is not an enlarged citation of BFJ.

\section{n4. New all-support geometric cone and envelope invariance for finite and specified infinite Frobenius-root extensions.}
\textit{Supports: G331, n1, n3.}

NEW Frobenius-root invariance on the entire geometric envelope class. Let R subset S be an injective finite local extension of excellent reduced positive-dimensional rings of characteristic p\textgreater{}0. Assume a fixed q=p\textasciicircum{}e satisfies s\textasciicircum{}q in R for EVERY s in S. This hypothesis holds when R is a normal domain, S a finite domain, and Frac(S)/Frac(R) is finite purely inseparable: choose q killing that extension; s\textasciicircum{}q lies in Frac(R), is integral over R and hence lies in R. No perfection, F-finiteness, CM, Q-Gorenstein or resolution hypothesis is imposed. This is a proved envelope transport theorem; it supplies no unproved positive-characteristic regularity input.

Fix a primary ideal a in R. Restriction rho:V\_(aS)(S)-\textgreater{}V\_a(R) is an actual homeomorphism with inverse

 w(s)=v(s\textasciicircum{}q)/q, including infinity/q=infinity.

The formula has w|R=v. Frobenius additivity (s+t)\textasciicircum{}q=s\textasciicircum{}q+t\textasciicircum{}q and multiplicativity prove the semivaluation axioms. Because aS is primary and w(aS)=1, the source maximal ideal has positive finite value. For example n\textasciicircum{}N subset mS subset n gives 0\textless{}w(m)/N\textless{}=w(n)\textless{}=w(m)\textless{}infinity. Supports are determined by s\textasciicircum{}q in supp(v), including nonminimal supports. Any lift must have this value by multiplicativity, so it is unique. Both maps are continuous by coordinate evaluations and scalar division, or by compact bijection. This proves full fibre compatibility rather than assuming generic inseparability controls every support.

The independently geometric classes C and D are isometrically identical under rho. One may invoke G331's full finite-injection positive descent since every fibre is singleton, but there is also an explicit ideal proof. If I=(s\_1,...,s\_l) is unit or proper primary in S, put J=(s\_1\textasciicircum{}q,...,s\_l\textasciicircum{}q) in R. Then v(J)=q w(I) on EVERY support. If I contains (aS)\textasciicircum{}N, raising any expression x=sum\_i s\_i u\_i for x in a\textasciicircum{}N to the qth power shows x\textasciicircum{}q in J, since all u\_i\textasciicircum{}q are in R. Thus J has radical m; it is proper when I is proper and is unit when I is unit. Hence the rational profile -w(I)/r is exactly the pullback of -v(J)/(qr). Primary ideal extension gives the reverse inclusion. Rational density and uniform closure identify C; actual decreasing C-nets identify D in both directions. Thus for EVERY pointwise obstacle psi, whenever the raw supremum is defined with the usual bottom convention,

 rho\textasciicircum{}*p\_R(psi)=p\_S(rho\textasciicircum{}*psi).

For finite continuous obstacles, a continuous greatest geometric envelope exists on one side iff it exists on the other. In particular the all-obstacle CE property, any genuine continuous-obstacle CE failure, and continuity of the full canonical approximation transfer both ways. No norm average or positivity of a USC regularization is used. This is the exact operator compatibility required for these Frobenius-root maps, without any Monge-Ampere/product assertion.

There is quantitative canonical transport, even though same-degree scalar extension of canonical ideals is NOT asserted. Let B\_m=A\_m\textasciicircum{}S(rho\textasciicircum{}*f) and A\_m=A\_m\textasciicircum{}R(f). Surjectivity gives A\_m=B\_m intersect R. Let J\_m be generated by qth powers of generators of B\_m. Then kappa\_m=-v(J\_m)/(qm) pulls back to -w(B\_m)/m and J\_m subset A\_(qm). If a source estimate 0\textless{}=p\_S-h\_m\textasciicircum{}S\textless{}=E/m holds, n1's residue grading with M\_1=||v(A\_1)|| gives, for k\textgreater{}=2q,

 0\textless{}=p\_R-h\_k\textasciicircum{}R\textless{}=E/floor(k/q)+qM\_1/k\textless{}=(2qE+qM\_1)/k.

This is a conditional rate with its source hypothesis explicit. Uniform source convergence alone suffices for uniform target convergence, either by the exact envelope homeomorphism and n1 or by the same residue argument. Frobenius exponent q is not identified with generic degree or a ramification/residue sum.

An infinite-root version is also proved. Suppose R subset S is an injective extension of excellent reduced local rings of characteristic p, S is the union of local finite R-subalgebras, and every element of S has SOME p-power in R. Assume S is Noetherian and the marked maximal ideal and primary gauge are the extensions of those of R, as in the scalar example below. Define w(s)=v(s\textasciicircum{}(p\textasciicircum{}e))/p\textasciicircum{}e using any admissible e. Passing to a common larger exponent proves independence of e and proves all axioms for sums and products. Each coordinate formula uses a finite exponent, so the inverse is continuous; centredness follows from the extended maximal ideal. Every support is retained and rho is again a boundary homeomorphism. Every finitely generated primary source ideal and finitely many coefficients witnessing a primary-power containment have a common Frobenius exponent. Raising those expressions as above gives a genuine primary ideal J in R with exact rational profile. Thus C and actual D again coincide isometrically and envelopes commute for ALL obstacles. There need not be one global q, so no global degree-rate constant is claimed.

For a geometrically integral geometrically normal rational-point finite-type local k-domain in characteristic p, S=R tensor\_k k\textasciicircum{}perf is such an infinite-root example. It is the corresponding Noetherian excellent normal local ring, with mS its maximal ideal. Finite purely inseparable coefficient extensions form local finite stages. Each element is a finite sum of tensors; choose one exponent sending all its finitely many coefficients into k, and Frobenius sends the element into R. Thus CE is equivalent over k and its perfect closure, even for imperfect k, with every support included. This reduces the imperfect-field issue for this geometrically normal finite-type class; it does not prove CE in positive characteristic over the perfect closure.

Combining n3's separable-field descent with this root theorem gives a precise further reduction. For such geometrically normal rational-point germs in characteristic p, pass first to k\textasciicircum{}s and then to its perfect closure, which is an algebraic closure of k. Root invariance identifies the latter two envelope problems, and n3 gives rho\textasciicircum{}*p\_R(f)=p\_(R tensor k\textasciicircum{}s)(rho\textasciicircum{}*f) on compatible obstacles. Therefore geometric-algebraic-closure CE implies downstairs CE at these exact hypotheses, without enlarging BFJ to characteristic p. Mixed-characteristic rings have no Frobenius-root formula of this kind and are not covered. General nonnormal/conductor envelope commutation remains conditional outside these uniquely lifting maps, and all literal radial distinctions remain unchanged.

Artifacts: n4.txt, distinct full n4.tex, standalone TeX, entire companion and scoped verdict. Rerun: python assemble\_delivery.py; python finite\_checks.py; python check\_delivery.py. Finite Frobenius arithmetic is diagnostic only; the all-support cone and operator claims are proved above relative to qualified G331 and own n1/n3.

\subsection*{Expanded root transport and its quantitative limit}
For $q=p^e$ and $S^q\subset R$, all supports obey
\[
 w(s)=q^{-1}v(s^q),\qquad
 v((s_1^q,\ldots,s_l^q))=q\,w((s_1,\ldots,s_l)).
\]
The primaryness proof raises actual module expressions, not just
generic field identities: if $x\in\mathfrak a^N$ is expressed as
$\sum s_i u_i$, then $x^q=\sum s_i^qu_i^q\in J$ in $R$.
Thus the transported profiles have actual primary Cartier presentations.
Under an explicitly assumed source bound, and $k\ge2q$,
\[
 0\le p_R-h_k^R\le
 \frac{E}{\lfloor k/q\rfloor}+\frac{qM_1}{k}
 \le\frac{2qE+qM_1}{k}.
\]
An infinite coefficient perfection need not have bounded exponent.
Each finite ideal still has one common exponent, giving the exact
cone/operator transport, but the display supplies no uniform infinite
extension rate without additional bounds.

By Book Theorem 70.1.4,\textbackslash{}nthe link normalized by an m-primary ideal a is the ﬁnite discrete set of normalization branches\textbackslash{}nq above the closed point, with point\textbackslash{}n\textbackslash{}n                                         ordq / ordq (a).\textbackslash{}n\textbackslash{}nChanging the primary ideal rescales this same branch set by the exact cocycle (Book Chap-\textbackslash{}nter 70, †). In particular, branches cannot be indexed only by irreducible components: the irre-\textbackslash{}nducible node in Book Example 70.1.4 has one minimal prime and two normalization branches.\textbackslash{}n    There is also an exact link-level ﬁnite-map formula. Book Proposition 70.1.2 applies to\textbackslash{}nan arbitrary ﬁnite local homomorphism of reduced Nagata curve germs, without injectivity.\textbackslash{}nWhen a source branch has ramiﬁcation index e over a target branch, both its order and the\textbackslash{}norder of the extended ideal aS acquire the factor e, so normalization cancels it. The resulting\textbackslash{}nrestriction maps are compatible with composition and change of primary ideal. They need\textbackslash{}nnot be surjective; this remains a link-level result.\textbackslash{}n    There is now also an exact conductor-controlled ideal-level range. Book Proposition 70.1.3\textbackslash{}nshows that every suﬃciently deep integral vector of branch orders is realized by one downstairs\textbackslash{}nnonzerodivisor generating an m-primary ideal. Consequently Book Theorem 70.1.5 identiﬁes\textbackslash{}nthe principal-ideal proﬁles with the strictly negative rational orthant, their uniform closure\textbackslash{}nwith all nonpositive functions on the ﬁnite link, and their raw obstacle envelope with min\{φ, 0\}.\textbackslash{}nThe monotone boundary is sharp: all nonpositive proﬁles admit increasing approximation\textbackslash{}nfrom below, whereas a proﬁle with a zero branch coordinate cannot be a decreasing limit of\textbackslash{}nprimary-ideal proﬁles. These conclusions concern only the explicitly deﬁned ideal-function\textbackslash{}nfamily.\textbackslash{}n    There is now also a complete order-theoretic calculus for the book-local ideal-closure can-\textbackslash{}ndidate. By Book Theorem 70.1.6, its continuous ideal-psh class is every ﬁnite nonpositive\textbackslash{}nfunction on the branch link, and its decreasing completion is every extended nonpositive func-\textbackslash{}ntion. Equivalently, it is the intrinsic cone of nonpositive degree-one homogeneous functions\textbackslash{}non the unnormalized branch-valuation rays. Changing the primary ideal merely changes the\textbackslash{}ncross-section and obeys an exact cocycle; ﬁnite local maps pull the class back, preserve prin-\textbackslash{}ncipal models, and commute with the envelope min\{φ, 0\}. Their pullback image is exactly the\textbackslash{}nﬁber-constant subclass; pullback is injective precisely for a surjective branch map and sur-\textbackslash{}njective precisely for an injective branch map, while surjective branch maps act isometrically\textbackslash{}non the continuous class. On the completed lattices, ﬁberwise supremum and inﬁmum give\textbackslash{}nexplicit left and right adjoints to pullback, with −∞ and zero as the two empty-ﬁber conven-\textbackslash{}ntions; the resulting source-side composites are the least ﬁber-constant majorant and greatest\textbackslash{}nﬁber-constant minorant, so their ﬁxed points are exactly the functions descending through\textbackslash{}n\textbackslash{}n                                               60\textbackslash{}n\textbackslash{}nthe branch map. The completed class retains the identically −∞ bottom element because a\textbackslash{}nnonsurjective branch map can pull a nonbottom function to it; this occurs explicitly in Book\textbackslash{}nExample 70.1.8 for the projection of a node onto one branch.\textbackslash{}n   The reverse direction is now exact for every ﬁnite map whose branch map is surjective.\textbackslash{}nFor a base branch q, set δq = dimKq (S ⊗R Kq ). Conversely, if a base branch is missed,\textbackslash{}npullback is not injective and hence cannot have any left inverse. Thus branch surjectivity is\textbackslash{}nthe exact retraction criterion.
